### On the Secrets of Nature.

[...changing] and excluding veil is called **Darkness** by Moses; after its removal, that matter, which had rested in a state of potentiality <note>original: "Vermögenheit," referring to the Aristotelian and alchemical concept of prime matter existing as a possibility before it takes on a physical form</note>, now attained the **form of water**. This has three grades: the purest and highest of these is called the **heavenly fire-spirit**, from whose elements all angels and saints consist, by means of their light-form. The middle grade, however, is called the **heavenly air-spirit**, from whose elements the stars are composed. Finally, the lowest grade is called the **earthly spirit**, or air—or in some passages of Holy Scripture, **wind**. The subtler part of this is called fire; the coarser part, however, is water; and the sediment and coarse part of the water is the **earth** itself, stained and veiled with thick darkness.

And herein consists all the **passive matter** <note>original: "leidend verhaltende Materie," meaning matter that does not act of itself but is shaped and influenced by higher forces</note> of the entire world, into which the **Light** has imprinted all its effects and properties. Namely, the invisible light worked within the invisible spirit to produce invisible creatures; and both the invisible and visible light stirred and moved within both the invisible and the visible and coarse spirit to represent visible bodies. And therefore, every creature has its hidden essence and manifest existence, and its both hidden and spiritual, as well as

### Part Three of the Concluding Work.

...manifest properties. We therefore make a distinction between the inner and outer man. From this are derived the powers in this or that animal, plant, and underground growth <note>original: "unterirdischen Gewächse"; in early modern science and alchemy, minerals and metals were often thought to "grow" in the earth like plants</note>, for the healing of the frailties and diseases of the human body, for natural magical operations, and for countless other things. 

From this it is evident that, although the manifest properties of things and the effects of bodies are displayed before our eyes and senses, we must not for that reason reject what does not fall within our senses but lies hidden and concealed within bodies; because, just as the most excellent part of the whole of creation is invisible—namely, that heavenly <term>fire-spirit</term> <note>original: "Feuergeist"; a concept describing the highest, most refined spiritual substance that acts as the source of light and life</note>, and its invisible spirits, namely the angels, and the whole invisible heavenly host, to whose existence the invisible light and a similar spirit worked together—so also the most glorious secrets of God and of nature are invisible and can only be recognized through the eyes of the understanding. 

Gracious God, how little do we, most wretched and ignorant humans, understand of such secrets, and yet how perverse we are in this our shameful stubbornness and resistance against the laws of knowledge, in that we [ourselves...]

...want to absolutely convince ourselves as if we knew and understood everything, when in truth absolutely nothing is revealed to us except that by which we physically live (which we also have in common with the animals), and we know God and His Word only from afar and through revealed experience. Therefore, the wisest *Solomon* frequently bestowed shameful ignorance with public disgrace, as its most worthy reward, when he declared man—who is indeed endowed with natural reason, but stained and darkened by coarse ignorance—to be the most miserable and wretched among all the other animals. 

But let us rather proceed to that degree of spirit or water <note>original: "Geistes- oder Wassersgrade"; refers to the hierarchical levels of elemental or spiritual refinement in early modern cosmology</note> which concerns us more closely and acts more upon us, and examine more precisely the effects of the airy nature, in which wonderful things lie hidden: namely, angels of all kinds, the formative essences of lower things, the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: "Wurzelfeuchte"; also known as *humidum radicale*, this was thought to be the essential, life-sustaining fluid found in every living creature, which acted as the internal fuel for life's "lamp"</note> of every living creature, the nourishment of coarse fire, wonderful aerial phenomena such as <term>will-o'-the-wisps</term> <note>original: "Irrwische"; also known as *ignis fatuus* or "fool's fire," these are ghost lights seen over bogs or marshes, often interpreted as spirits in folklore</note>, etc., violent storms from all directions, and countless other secrets. To provide a sufficient explanation for all of these would be one of the most difficult *Labors of Hercules*. Therein consists (if I am not mistaken) the entire oc-

### The Third Part of the Concluding Manuscript.

...drums covered in wolf skins [cause] those [drums] covered with sheepskins <unclear>instruments?</unclear> to resonate quite audibly along with them. <note>This refers to a popular early modern belief in "antipathy" or "sympathy" between animals even after death; here, it is argued that the sound of a wolf-skin drum would affect a sheep-skin drum due to their natural relationship as predator and prey, though the author is grounding this in the emerging science of acoustics.</note> It is also known that one sounding organ pipe causes another, of the same pitch, to tremble. All of this is according to the truth and confirmed by experience. See Mizler's *Musical Library*, Vol. 1, Part 2, page 42. <note>original: "Mizlers musikal. Bibliothek"; Lorenz Christoph Mizler (1711–1778) was a prominent German physician and music theorist who sought to base musical theory on mathematics and science.</note>

> "If, for example, a specific tone is bowed or sounded on a violin in a room, then that string on the piano standing in the same room, which is tuned to the same degree as the string on the violin producing said tone, will also sound the same tone. And why should this not be able to occur? When the air, shaken by a sound, strikes a body whose particles and fibers are tensioned to such a degree that they are capable of receiving this kind of vibration, then these fibers also enter into a similar motion; which is suited to excite the same sound and tone in the adjacent air. The soundboard of instruments confirms this. From this, various phenomena can be explained. Among these I include the case of that mathematician at Aix in Provence in the year 1664. He placed a skeleton in the middle of the room between the window and the door,"

The artist sat opposite the window and began, when the air was bright and pure, to play upon his zither, the strings of which were tuned exactly like those he had attached to the skeleton. ### Third Part of the Defense.

...perceives.) For in all the previously mentioned effects, spiritual natures—whether they be of a more **earthly-airy** or **heavenly-airy** <note>original: "lustiger"; in the 18th century, this term was often used as an adjective for the element of Air (Luft), implying something vibrant, gaseous, or vital, rather than just "joyful."</note> and ethereal kind—are stimulated and driven by the **invisible light**, and they repel opposing natures from themselves; just as, conversely, like-minded natures tend to attract and embrace similar ones through a **fiery and harmonious bond of love**. For love and hate are also found, according to our understanding, even in inanimate things; <note>The author is referring to the ancient philosophical concept that "Love" (attraction) and "Strife" (repulsion) are the fundamental forces of the universe, acting on magnets and minerals just as they do on people.</note> hence it happens that when the **sphere of influence** <term>Wirkungskreis: the physical or spiritual field within which a being or object exerts its power</term> of one creature corresponds with the **light-particles** of another in a good and well-tuned proportion, a constant harmony and peace reigns between them; if, however, the proportion of the one is unequal, and does not harmonize in one tone and concord with the other, then nothing but war, enmity, and aversion rules between them. 

If we descend even deeper into the nature of the **airy sphere of influence**, we will find that it is the proportion of celestial influences, and preserves within itself a **heavenly treasury of air**, <note>original: "Luftschatz"; likely referring to the "Vital Air" or "Aether" that alchemists believed carried life-force from the stars to the Earth.</note> from which it bountifully communicates to other elements and things composed of elements. From this we see that images, forms, and all species—

# Third Part of the Concluding Treatise.
<meta>catchword: So</meta>

...of natural and artificial things, just as fishes swim around in the visible, coarse sea. From this we can judge how far the secrets of this general <term>sphere of action</term> <note>original: "Würkungskreis"; the field of energy or atmosphere through which both spiritual and natural forces exert their influence.</note> extend, and what wonderful effects, according to its natural properties, may appear within it. These effects, since they exceed our <term>intellectual power</term> <note>original: "Denkungskraft"; the human capacity for reasoning and comprehension.</note>, must certainly seem to the ignorant to be nothing other than extremely difficult or even impossible. If we consider all of this back and forth with the greatest possible shrewdness, we will see that it is quite possible that someone, by means of this clear and bright general sphere of action, could converse with someone several hundred miles away within a few hours, both orally and in writing. However, because this attempt can be performed in many ways—falsified in part through forbidden <term>sorcery</term> <note>original: "Zauberey."</note> and in part through <term>necromancy</term> <note>original: "Todtenbeschwörung"; the practice of communicating with the spirits of the dead to gain hidden knowledge.</note> *)—I will here briefly explain its true nature.

### On Secrets of Nature.

Both credible history and arguments drawn from natural and supernatural <term>philosophy</term> <note>original: "Weltweisheit"; a historical German term for philosophy, literally meaning "world-wisdom."</note> confirm that the aforementioned task and intention of the Brothers <note>Likely referring to the Rosicrucian Brotherhood or a similar esoteric society mentioned in the preceding chapters.</note> can be successfully achieved and desirably carried out in two ways: namely, either through divine inspiration and <term>enlightenment</term> <note>original: "Erleuchtung"; the state of receiving spiritual or intellectual insight from a divine source.</note>, or through the help of purely natural art. 

For it is recorded that when the radiance of divine light pours out and spreads as a reflection upon someone through a specially granted grace, this person can see, recognize, and address another who is many hundreds of miles away through this beam of light. Indeed, by means of this very radiance, they can even receive explicit answers and speeches from them, such that it seems to them as if the one they are conversing with were quite near at hand. 

It is also proven from Holy Scripture that the prophets and other pious men, in the radiance of the Holy Spirit, are said to have seen what occurred, was deliberated upon, and spoken in the most distant places, and that they notified princes and kings in remote locations of these things and warned them in advance of the plots of their enemies. Therefore, these prophets—

## On the possibility that someone who can read only a single book may simultaneously understand everything that has ever been described in all books.

## The Sixth Chapter.

### On the Hidden and Wondrous Effects of Secret Music.

Text: Whoever desires to learn vocal or instrumental music in such a way that he draws to himself—not like Amphion <note>In Greek mythology, Amphion's music was so powerful it caused stones to move of their own accord to build the walls of Thebes.</note>, the rocks, but rather pearls and precious stones; and not like Orpheus <note>A legendary musician whose song could charm wild beasts and even persuade the gods of the underworld.</note>, the wild beasts, but rather spirits; and not Pluto <note>The Roman ruler of the underworld; the original text mentions "drawing Pluto out of hell," referring to the Orpheus myth.</note> out of hell, but rather the mighty princes of the world—let him enter into the brotherhood.

If we can give any credit to the opinions of Plato and Cardanus <note>Girolamo Cardano (1501–1576), an Italian polymath who wrote on the "music of the world" and occult resonances.</note>, the most beautiful harmony is produced from the conjunction of the spheres or globes <note>This refers to the "Music of the Spheres" (musica universalis), the ancient theory that the movements of the planets create a divine, mathematical harmony inaudible to the human ear.</note>; which, because of the great distance, cannot indeed be heard by our ears. # The Third Part of the Defense.

...to show the reasons *), which is why Saturn—although cold and dry by nature—becomes accidentally evil and unlucky because it ends in complete agreement with the element of fire, which is warm. Likewise, Mars, which is warm and dry, pours its entire influence into the cold and moist element of water; thus they become harmful through these circumstances, even though they are intrinsically as good as the other planets, the Sun excepted. 

Conversely, Jupiter, which is warm and moist, aligns in perfect agreement with the element of its own nature, namely air. Venus also harmonizes with this in her **fifth**, **diapente**, <note>original: "Quinte, Diapente". Both terms refer to the musical interval of a "fifth," which was considered a perfect and harmonious ratio in the Pythagorean "music of the spheres."</note> or a balanced ratio of one-fifth. Consequently, all creatures living beneath the moon that enjoy the influence of these good planets embrace one another out of love and natural agreement. On the other hand, those creatures that have taken in the hostile impressions of the "evil" stars feel a deep loathing for one another. 

Thus we find that, for example, the wolf has a <term>martial</term> <note>Influenced by the planet Mars; characterized by aggression, hostility, or a warlike spirit.</note> and hostile nature, while the lamb has a <term>jovial</term> <note>Influenced by the planet Jupiter; characterized by a peaceful, calm, and benevolent temperament.</note> and peaceful one. For in the hidden treasure chest of nature <term>treasure chest of nature: A common metaphorical concept in early modern science and alchemy representing the storehouse of God's hidden laws and physical wonders.</term> there are countless locked secrets which are not revealed to the common man; however, through their revelation, once it has occurred, an innumerable multitude of secrets are discovered for us. Of these, I wish to mention only a single little treasure box of nature, which indeed appears very small to the senses, but beneath whose visible shell I have found wonderful secrets of the most important things hidden; of which I shall treat more extensively in the following chapter.

## In the Microcosm, in Animals and Plants.
<note>The "Little World" (original: "kleine Welt") refers to the concept of the Microcosm, where individual living beings are seen as a smaller reflection of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note>

This essence is driven toward enlargement and multiplication by the ethereal or heavenly-airy spirit, together with the light hidden within it. 
This capacity, this power, is likely what the **Stoics** <note>An ancient Greek school of philosophy that taught that the universe is permeated by a rational, divine "breath" or spirit called pneuma.</note> understood when they explained the soul as a fiery being <note>original: "Feuerwesen"</note> transformed into water through the medium of air.

### In the Microcosm: In Animals and Plants.

## Author’s Concluding Address
### to the Brothers
## of the Rosy Cross.

You promise, most beloved Brothers, to restore—through communal aid—those spiritual human forms and their eternal blessings <note>The author refers to the Hermetic and mystical belief that humanity originally possessed a divine, spiritual body that was lost or "corrupted" after the biblical Fall.</note> which were transformed into wretched images through the unfortunate Fall of Adam. Accordingly, I revere you in Jesus Christ, the Savior of the world, whom you serve with a pure and sincere heart.

Forgive me if I—as a still ignorant worldly philosopher <note>original: *Weltweise*; a term used for secular scholars or academics who lack spiritual illumination.</note> and unworthy confessor of your fame—have committed an error or a scientific mistake in this little work. And since I desire nothing more, nor more dearly, than to be the lowliest member in the society of your Order, so that I might satisfy the curious ears of men through a more worthy and reliable spreading of your fame, allow me to briefly indicate who I am: I have listed my birth, my rank, residence, and name above. I am of noble

noble lineage; my wife is named: **Desire for Wisdom** <note>original: *Weisheitsbegierde*; likely a reference to Sophia (Wisdom), whom many Christian mystics and alchemists personified as a spiritual bride.</note>; my children are the fruits produced and arising from her; my body is a **prison**; the pleasures of the world are vain and harmful to the soul <note>original: *Gemüthe*; refers to one's internal disposition or spiritual temperament.</note>. I have experienced and successfully survived the stormy deep sea, the steep mountains, the slippery valleys, the ignorance in the countryside, and the coarseness in cities, the haughtiness and pride of citizens, greed, infidelity, ignorance, laziness, persecution, and nearly all human misfortune; and I have encountered no one who has achieved the highest state of happiness, or who has rightly known himself, as the holy **John I** speaks: 

> The life was the light, and this light was in the darkness; but the darkness did not understand it. <note>A translation of the German rendering of John 1:4-5: "Das Leben war das Licht, und dieses Licht war in der Finsterniß; aber die Finsterniß hat es nicht begriffen."</note>

Everywhere (I say) one encounters pure vanity, and everything is, as it were, nothing other than pure misery and vanity itself. And herewith I wish to commend myself to you. Fare you well,

# Concerning Secret Designations. #

...extent, is to be held as a vain and superstitious science, as many ill-intentioned people believe. However, that it is a science which explains the causes of past events and the <term>prophecy<note>The original German 'Weissagung' refers to the act of foretelling or divining future events through natural or divine signs.</note></term> of future things—derived from the relative positions of the stars, their <term>aspects<note>In astrology, an 'aspect' is the specific angular distance between two planets, which practitioners believe influences human affairs.</note></term> and secret properties, and even from hidden designations regarding their natural placement and form—cannot be unknown to anyone who is properly versed in the true art of <term>astrology<note>original: 'Sterndeuterkunst'</note></term>. Thus, this science is in and of itself undoubtedly true, certain, and most highly to be recommended among all the <term>liberal arts<note>The 'liberal arts' (Latin: artes liberales) were the traditional branches of knowledge, such as rhetoric and astronomy, considered essential for a free person to master.</note></term>. Yet, insofar as it is corrupted by the various practices of the practitioner, to that extent it becomes uncertain, superstitious, and in some measure damnable. For this reason, the ancients generally divided this entire science—which was only tainted by the aforementioned causes—into many distinct branches. One such branch is purely "natural" astrology, which deals with public fortune and the general state of affairs, using means and causes recognized and taken from the innermost depths of nature.

This branch examines the state, the fortune, and the public condition; examples of this include: the changes in the air, in regards to whether it is healthy, disease-inducing, or pestilen-

## Chapter Four.

### The Various Forms of Holy Scripture; and in What Manner and Way It Appears in Print.

---

> Text from page 73 of the Confession <note>The *Confessio Fraternitatis*, one of the founding manifestos of the Rosicrucian Order, published in 1615.</note>: From the Holy Scriptures and the markings <term>signatures; the theory that the outward appearance of things in nature reveals their hidden properties</term> in the great world <note>The macrocosm, or the universe at large.</note> is derived the magical script and new language, through which the nature of all things is explained. Whoever, therefore, possesses knowledge of such a language can also understand the signatures of nature.

Just as I divided the divine books into the invisible and the visible in the previous chapter, in the very same way, I also distinguish their writings—namely, into the visible and the invisible. -># On Secret Markings.<-

In this treatise, I have merely intended to explain something of the secret script on the visible pages of the great Book of Nature, which certainly—given my own ignorance—is to be seen as nothing more than the mere shadow of the thing itself, or the surface of the truth.

The visible script of nature is thus printed clearly enough, partly on artificial surfaces—namely stone ones and the like—and partly on natural pages, namely in the great Book of Nature. In the preceding sections, it has been proven that the secret designations <note>The author uses "Bezeichnungen" to refer to the "Doctrine of Signatures"—the belief that God marked all of creation with physical signs that reveal their hidden purpose or nature.</note> were imprinted and inscribed into the stars by God on the fourth day <note>A reference to the biblical creation account in Genesis 1:14–19, where God creates the sun, moon, and stars to serve as "signs."</note>. Without the essential knowledge, reading, and true meaning of these designations and secret symbols, all worldly wisdom is nothing other than true folly.

For Jupiter, being more powerful than Saturn, is a good omen for the world, namely the arrival of <term>prophets</term>, both according to the natural character of the "house" <note>In astrology, a "house" is one of the twelve sections of the sky that govern different areas of life.</note> and by virtue of the auspicious conjunction which, according to <term>Masha'allah</term> <note>original: "Messahalah," an 8th-century Persian-Jewish astrologer whose work was foundational to European medieval astrology.</note>, signifies the arrival of one or more prophets—and indeed no false, deceitful, or evil ones, but good ones, according to the qualities of the planets that have come together in a favorable constellation

# The Second Part of the Defense.
## Regarding the Obstacles of Contemporary Scholastic Sciences. <note>original: *Schulwissenschaften*. This refers to the traditional university curriculum and the established academic methods of the era, which the Rosicrucians sought to reform.</note>

---

### The First Chapter.
#### Concerning the Content of this Second Part.

I have composed the second section of my defense for the purpose of examining the authenticity of the second line of reasoning put forth by the Society of the Rose Cross <note>The Rosicrucians, a secret brotherhood whose manifestos in the early 1600s called for a "universal reformation" of mankind, science, and religion.</note> on the scales of truth. This likely refers to Libavius's *Analysis confessionis Fraternitatis de Rosea Cruce* (1615), where he analyzed the Rosicrucian claims.</note> with these words: "One should join the Society of the Rose Cross; because it has discovered the deficiencies of our previous academic arts; <note>original: *Schul-Künste*. These are the traditional Liberal Arts taught in schools, which the reformers argued had become hollow and impractical.</note> furthermore, the remedy for them; thirdly, the certain signs of the following centuries; fourthly, the motivating factors as to why the coming times"

# Obstacles of Scholastic Sciences #

### The Second Part of the Defense ###

...illuminated by sparks from the brilliance of true philosophy <term>Weltweisheit; literally "world-wisdom," an 18th-century term for philosophy that included the natural sciences</term>, [one] can bring forth roses and ripe grapes in the month of March. Likewise, they have caused planted beans or sown parsley to sprout into a complete plant within a few hours, and other such things. Basel, 1593. Yet how often does experience deceive astrologers, physiognomists, and physicians? For knowledge of things derived from experience is uncertain; but it is safe and certain when derived from rational deductions <note>original: *Vernunftschlüssen*; the author argues that true "Magic" is a rational system of causes, not just trial-and-error observation</note>. For the purpose or effect of a natural thing is recognized even if one does not yet know its cause; as some things are better known and more certain to us because they are more present in reality and concern the specific and corporeal more directly;

# Obstacles of the Academic Sciences #

...and others, on the other hand, belong more to Nature, which are namely more general and incorporeal. These first natural principles therefore appear dark and confused to us because they are better known to Nature herself. Individual empirical conclusions <note>Conclusions drawn from specific observations or trials</note> very often deceive; general rational deductions <note>Conclusions based on universal logical principles</note> never do. Secret philosophy <note>original: *Weltweisheit*; literally "world-wisdom," used here to mean occult or metaphysical philosophy</note> will present Nature in her nakedness and contemplate the wisdom of general forces with the eyes of the intellect; common philosophy, it is true, is accustomed to taste from the side-streams of the source of light, but it can provide no cause for the crystalline waters, their clarity, or their becoming cloudy and thick. 

Therefore, I maintain that sciences which base themselves solely on experience are to be considered uncertain, deceptive, and inauthentic. From this arise the errors in astrology, physiognomy <note>The study of a person's character or fortune based on their facial features</note>, and medicine <note>original: *Arzneywissenschaft*</note>. Even *Dr. Libavius* <note>Andreas Libavius (c. 1555–1616) was a German physician and chemist. While he believed in the possibility of alchemy, he was a staunch critic of the mystical and "magical" language used by Paracelsus and his followers.</note> will have to confess that this art requires a refinement and a "remelting" <note>original: "Umschmelzung"; a metaphor for purging the art of its impurities, much like refining metal in a furnace.</note>. For since he has worked for so many years in the workshop of *Vulcan* <note>Vulcan was the Roman god of fire; in alchemical texts, his "workshop" or "forge" is a standard metaphor for the chemical laboratory.</note>, he has undoubtedly learned the difficulty of this gilded science from his own experience, and has untiringly tracked down the disgusting and tasteless lies of the writings at his own expense. It is indeed to be wondered at how those experienced in this science could make use of so many fabricated words—so far removed from the truth of the art—through which they wished to hide the truth and its secrets from the "unworthy and the fools" (as they express it). These terms include: *Sulfur* and *Mercury*, *Calcination* <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder.</note>, *Separation*, *Union*, *Putrefaction* <note>The stage of "rot" or decomposition in the alchemical process, usually associated with the color black.</note>, *Congelation*, *Fermentation*, *Multiplication*, *Imbibition*, *Ceration* <note>original: "Eintränkung in Wachs"; the process of softening a chemical substance until it reaches a wax-like consistency.</note>, *Projection*, *Exaltation*, the *Green Lion* <note>A famous alchemical symbol, often representing a powerful solvent like nitric acid or a specific mineral like antimony.</note>, *White Smoke*, and countless other deceptive technical terms through which people are deceived and their gold purses are miserably plundered. Even the more sincere chemical writers confess this in their writings. ### # Obstacles to the Scholastic Sciences

To philosophers, this masterpiece <note>The "masterpiece" (original: *Meisterstück*) refers to the *Opus Magnum* or the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> is hidden and concealed, and where it is indeed encountered, it is called by a thousand different names; it is also sealed, and open only to the wise. And yet it is but one thing, a single and direct path toward its perfection. 

And (according to my insight) neither common fire, but rather natural fire <note>In alchemy, "natural fire" refers to an internal, spiritual, or essential heat, as opposed to the "vulgar fire" of a physical furnace.</note>, nor artificial furnaces, but rather natural vessels and containers, are required for this work; for it is purely a work of nature, requiring only a small assistance from the servant <note>The "servant" is the alchemist himself, who, according to the Hermetic tradition, is merely a "minister of nature" (*minister naturae*) who assists natural processes rather than forcing them.</note>, so that the natural things that agree with one another in their kind may be properly processed. 

I therefore conclude from this: that in natural philosophy <note>original: *Weltweisheit*; literally "world-wisdom," the contemporary term for the study of the natural world.</note>, medical science, and alchemy—as these sciences are generally understood and presented to the public—a reformation and improvement (if indeed any such thing is possible) is both necessary and to be expected. It would therefore be absurd, and deserving of punishment with the whip of ignorance, if we

At the heart of the image is an oval frame containing a <term>Pelican in her piety</term> (a white pelican pecking its breast to nourish its young with its own blood). This is a powerful symbol in both Christian art and alchemy, where it represents sacrifice, the "Great Work" of purification, and the life-giving power of the <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term>. 

The pelican sits atop a white cubic altar. On the front of the altar is a <term>Rose Cross</term>—a diagonal red cross with four red roses—identifying this work with the Rosicrucian movement, a secret society of "learned brothers" seeking spiritual and social reformation. Above the scene, a golden sun shines, symbolizing divine light or the completion of the alchemical process. The entire emblem is encircled by a golden border bearing the title of the work.</note>

# Preface
## of the Translator.

> O how many things are there still in heaven
> and on earth, of which our entire philosophy
> lets itself not even dream!
> ->Shakespeare’s Hamlet.<-
<note>original: "O wie viel Dinge giebt es nicht noch im Himmel und auf Erden, wovon unſre ganze Philoſophie ſich nichts träumen läßt!" This is a German translation of the famous line from Act 1, Scene 5: "There are more things in heaven and earth, Horatio, Than are dreamt of in your philosophy."</note>

---

The reader should expect no extensive apology or justification from me here for this undertaken work. I believe I have far less need of this than our contemporary novelists <term>Romanenschreiber; literally "romance-writers," referring to authors of popular fiction which, in the 18th century, was often viewed as frivolous compared to scholarly or spiritual works</term> and translators. At the very least, I can say this much: that it has not occurred without request and prompting,

->*<-

He asserts that his work is necessary and was produced in response to a specific demand, contrasting it with common popular fiction.</summary>

--- Page 177 ---
<meta>continuity: catchword "und" (and) connects the previous page's mention of the work being commissioned to this page's hope for its usefulness.</meta>

and that it will, hopefully, also not be entirely without use. The Latin original of it is—just like *Fludd’s* <note>original: "Fluds." Robert Fludd (1574–1637) was a prominent English physician and mystical philosopher whose works combined science, medicine, and occultism.</note> works in general—extremely rare, especially because it was not printed together with them in folio format <term>Bogen-format; large-sized books where printed sheets are folded only once</term>, but separately in *octavo* <term>Octav; a smaller book size where sheets are folded into eight leaves</term> at *Leiden* in 1617. Consequently, even in the most complete collections of *Fludd’s* works, which I have indeed seen brought together several times, this present treatise was always missing. For this reason alone, it deserved to be sought out and made better known through a German translation. But its uncommon and extraordinary content will also justify my undertaking. Lovers of natural science and researchers of higher secrets of nature will read it not without instruction and not without delight; and perhaps many who are eager for knowledge and wisdom will encounter something in it that will capture their attention and stimulate and guide them toward further reflection and research. For:

> “If the <term>reason-wise</term> <note>original: "Vernunftweisen." ### of the Translator. ###

* 3

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

As for the matter itself—namely the Order of the <term>Rosicrucians</term> <note>original: "Rosenkreuzer." A legendary and secretive spiritual and cultural movement that arose in early 17th-century Europe, claiming to possess esoteric wisdom passed down from antiquity.</note> which prompted this treatise—it has indeed commonly been presented in history as an old fable, or at least as a secret riddle. This has given rise to various and peculiar judgments among both the learned and the unlearned, and to a multitude of writings for and against it (the most excellent of which have been pointed out in the appropriate places). Indeed, very many have wanted to declare it flatly a <term>chimera</term> <note>original: "Chimäre." In this context, a "chimera" refers to a baseless fabrication or an impossible figment of the imagination.</note> and an absurdity. However, from this uncertainty, from this error, a member of this Order has now recently and suddenly rescued us, and through his writing—**The Rosicrucian in His Nakedness;** <note>original: "Der Rosenkreuzer in seiner Blöße." Published in 1781, this was a famous "exposure" or critique of the Gold- und Rosenkreuzer order, written by Hans Heinrich von Ecker und Eckhoffen under the pseudonym Magister Pianco.</note> by Magister Pianco, associate of many circles; Amsterdam 1781, <term>octavo</term> <note>original: "8." Refers to the book size, where each sheet is folded into eight leaves.</note>—has at least informed us of the external certainty of his Order and of its existence. Regarding its inner value and other qualities, it is not our place to judge further.

<summary>The translator acknowledges that while the Rosicrucian Order was long dismissed as a myth or a "chimera," a recent 1781 publication by "Magister Pianco" has proven its physical existence, even if its spiritual value remains a matter of debate.</summary>

# Foreword #

<meta>catchword: point</meta>

The author indeed expresses himself as being very dissatisfied with this. But perhaps he was seeking something more, or perhaps something entirely different, than what he should have been seeking? Was his effort perhaps directed toward something as an ultimate goal which he should have regarded only as a means to an end? Or did he perhaps even demand a <term>gold-making process</term> <note>original: "Goldmacherproceß." The alchemical practice of attempting to transmute base metals into gold, often a point of contention between those seeking material wealth and those seeking spiritual transformation.</note>, and that the entire <term>Secret of the Order</term> <note>original: "Geheimniß des Ordens." The core esoteric knowledge of a secret society, often claimed to be incommunicable through language.</note> should be told and revealed to him literally? This could not be done, and presumably, no one had promised to do so—or if they had, certainly not literally or directly, but only figuratively and from a distance.

For, what if the whole matter cannot be said, cannot be expressed in words, but must be personally experienced and felt? What if the initiate merely becomes infatuated with the <term>external</term> <note>original: "Aeusserliche." The superficial aspects of the Order, such as rituals, ceremonies, or material promises.</note> and remains stuck there, never coming to know the <term>inner reality</term> <note>original: "das Innere." The spiritual or esoteric core of the teaching.</note> or the <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Wesen." The fundamental nature of the society's wisdom.</note> itself; only constantly wandering about on the outer surface and never [approaching] the center—

<summary>The translator defends the Rosicrucian Order against critics who may have misunderstood its spiritual goals, suggesting that esoteric truths must be experienced rather than explained literally. He questions whether critics were distracted by a desire for material alchemy or outward rituals.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, gold-making, Secret of the Order, spiritual experience, internal vs. external, essence, Rosicrucians</keywords>

### of the Translator. ###

...points? Then it is certainly not possible otherwise: he is deceived, or rather, he deceives himself! But it is also just as possible, or rather probable, that it happened to the aforementioned author as it did to those <term>Freemasons</term> <note>original: "Freymaurer." Members of the fraternal organization known for its secret rituals and symbols.</note> who moved from one false <term>lodge</term> <note>original: "Loge." A local chapter or branch of the Masonic fraternity.</note> to another; were received from one false <term>Order</term> <note>original: "Orden." An organized secret society, often with a hierarchical structure.</note> into another, and were deceived time and time again. The history of this is well worth reading in that fine work: **On the Purpose of the Masonic Order. Germany 1781. Octavo.** <note>original: "Ueber den Zweck des Freymaurerordens. Germanien 1781. 8." A significant anonymous work—often attributed to August Gottlieb Meißner or Johann Joachim Christoph Bode—that critiqued the state of contemporary Freemasonry.</note> For I have always thought, as even <term>Falk</term> <note>Likely Johannes Daniel Falk or a contemporary Masonic figure quoted in the literature of the time.</note> quite correctly says: "who does not initiate; and who is not initiated!" <note>original: "wer nimmt nicht auf; und wer wird nicht aufgenommen!" A rhetorical question reflecting on the excessive growth and lack of discernment in 18th-century secret societies.</note>

What, then, is my conclusion from all of this; or what is my perspective aiming at? Should it follow from this that there is nothing at all to the whole matter? That would be a very unphilosophical, indeed an entirely irrational, judgment. Does the <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Wesen." The fundamental reality or spiritual truth of the practice.</note> of a thing and its existence depend upon its <term>ceremonies</term> <note>original: "Ceremonien." The formal rituals and external performances of the group.</note>; or <term>Freemasonry</term> <note>original: "Freymaurerey." Here distinguished from the "Order" as the pure philosophical ideal versus the flawed human institution.</note> upon the Order?

### ->Preface<- ###

...ter; or is it not rather the other way around—the former being deceived by the latter? We all know, after all, that the abuse of a thing does not abolish its true and proper use. <note>A reference to the Latin legal and philosophical maxim: "Abusus non tollit usum."</note> Or should misunderstandings, incorrect concepts and notions, prejudices, and the ignorance of most people regarding these matters be able to provide proof that all true insight and knowledge of these things is entirely impossible and not to be found in any human being? 

And finally, were there, for example, no merits in the world before the establishment of the **Order *for Merit*** <note>original: "pour le Merite." A famous Prussian order of merit established by Frederick the Great in 1740.</note>? Or would merit indeed cease to exist if the Order of Merit were no longer there? Were there not, and must there not have been, <term>Freemasons</term> and <term>Rosicrucians</term> <note>original: "Roſenkreuzer." A spiritual and cultural movement that arose in the early 17th century, claiming to possess ancient esoteric wisdom.</note> before their Orders existed? 

Yes, even more: are there not still today true Freemasons and genuine Rosicrucians who have never been admitted into the Orders? And those who are so in spirit, without being called by the name? And on the other hand, are there not very many who have been admitted into the Orders—who are lodge-compliant <note>original: "logenmäßige." Referring to members who follow the outward organizational rules and rituals of a Masonic lodge but may lack the internal spiritual understanding.</note>...

<summary>The translator argues that the true essence of Freemasonry and Rosicrucianism exists independently of their formal organizations, using the "Order of Merit" as an analogy for how virtues exist before and after the institutions that celebrate them.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the translator’s argument against the elitist notion that the general public should be kept in ignorance. It builds on the rhetorical question started on the previous page regarding whether error is "beneficial" for the common person.</meta>

### ->from the Translator.<- ###

<meta>catchword: it</meta>

...it would not be more beneficial for the **common man** to be kept in **error**?" For where is there a creature who does not yearn more for **light** than for **darkness**! Which spirit does not strive for **perfection**! Which rational human wishes to be blind for their entire life, and does not prefer the day to the night! Which person—no matter how **pleasantly lost** <note>original: "angenehm Verirrte." This refers to someone who is blissfully ignorant or comfortable in their misconceptions, yet would still theoretically prefer the truth if shown it.</note>—does not thank most heartily the one who has set them right! For "generally, one should fight only against, and seek to suppress, that which diminishes the truly good in the world, sows the seeds of dangerous errors that dishonor and destroy **humanity**, and makes the practice or application of the **truth** more difficult." For to be able to err is indeed human; but to *want* to err, to remain in old error, to keep others in error, or to want to lead others into new and more numerous errors, is—whether it happens under some spiritual and holy, or political and worldly **deception** <note>original: "Betruge." The author suggests that keeping people in ignorance is a form of fraud, whether committed by the church ("spiritual") or the state ("political").</note>—

<summary>The translator argues that every human being naturally yearns for truth and perfection, rejecting the idea that the lower classes are better off being "kept in error." He defines the intentional preservation of ignorance as a harmful deception against humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Enlightenment, truth vs. error, education, human rights, deception, social progress</keywords>

--- Page 187 ---
<meta>This page continues the translator's argument against the "devilish" practice of keeping the public in ignorance. It picks up mid-sentence from the previous page's discussion on spiritual or political deception.</meta>

### ->Foreword<- ###

<meta>catchword: however-</meta>

...however it may be, it is devilish! The Apostle <note>Referring to Saint Paul</note> in Romans 3:8 considers it a damned blasphemy if one were to say: let us do evil, so that good may come of it. <note>original: "lasset uns übel thun, auf daß gutes daraus komme." The author is citing the biblical principle that a good end (stability or "blissful ignorance") does not justify the evil means of deception.</note> Therefore, he who contributes something to the rooting out of errors and prejudices helps to spread light over darkness—lets day follow the night—opens eyes—saves souls! What true **friend of humanity** <note>original: "Menschenfreund." A central term in the 18th-century Enlightenment, describing an individual motivated by a rational love for their fellow man and a desire for social progress.</note> does not feel himself penetrated here by the innermost joy of the heart, and animated with the liveliest feeling of the most joyful gratitude toward God—the immeasurable **source of light**—in order to establish a divinely blessed monument to His most worthy instrument, ***Joseph II***, <note>Joseph II (1741–1790) was the Holy Roman Emperor and a prominent "enlightened despot" who enacted sweeping reforms in education, religion, and civil rights in the Habsburg lands.</note> the most wise, most kind ruler and father of his states, the brilliant spreader of light, the great savior of so many present and future souls! O happy age, which beholds Him as the most sublime and venerable model in the powerful rooting out of old and deeply rooted and...

# Introduction.

Upon the most precise and diligent consideration of the **hardships** <note>original: *Ungemächlichkeiten*; refers to the physical and spiritual discomforts or trials of mortal life</note> of this present life, which seldom find their end any sooner than through a blessed death, I have observed: that this **earthly pilgrimage** <note>original: *irrdische Wallfarth*; a common contemporary metaphor for life as a spiritual journey toward a holy destination</note> of mankind may not improperly be compared to a sea troubled by storming and raging waves. On this sea, the body—like a ship tossed to and fro by the uncertain waves of the deep—hovers in constant danger; but the soul is similar to a completely inexperienced **helmsman** <note>original: *Steuermann*</note>, who does not know how to arrive at the right time with his ship in the unknown **harbor of happiness** <term>Glückseligkeit</term> <note>This term refers to a state of supreme blessedness or spiritual fulfillment, rather than simple temporal joy</note>. From this it follows that among many thousands of people, hardly one keeps his course directed toward the desired goal of happiness. However, that people, even in this world, are able to arrive at a certain and infallible happiness, and

## Introduction.

... Berlin 1778. octavo. 1st Part p. 57–60. And simultaneously with a detailed explanation from **Herman Fictuld's Assembly of the Philosophers** <term>Turba Philosophorum</term> <note>Herman Fictuld was a pseudonym for a prolific 18th-century German alchemist and member of the Gold- and Rosicrucian Order; the *Turba* is one of the most famous early Latin alchemical texts</note>, also located there in the fourth part from p. 17 to p. 39. It is also very beautifully printed, both in German and in its original language—namely with the Phoenician text—together with a short critical history of **Hermes**, found in the preface to the beautiful latest edition of the **Golden Chain of Homer** <term>Aureae catenae Homeri</term> <note>A fundamental alchemical text that describes the interconnectedness of all nature from the divine down to the mineral</note>, enriched with useful notes, under the new title: **Plato's Ring: or, a Physical and Chemical Explanation of Nature.** <term>Annulus Platonis</term> Berlin and Leipzig 1781. large octavo. In this regard, one should generally compare the preface to the **Compass of the Wise** <term>Compaß der Weisen</term> <note>Another significant Rosicrucian-Alchemical work from 1779</note>, p. 32 f.

The other work of **Hermes** we have recently received in our language under the following title: **Hermes Trismegistus's Poemander, or on Divine Power and Wisdom** <note>The *Poemander* (or Pymander) is the first and most famous treatise in the *Corpus Hermeticum*, a collection of Greco-Egyptian wisdom texts</note>, translated from the Greek and accompanied by notes by **Dieterich Tiedemann**. Berlin and Stettin, 1781. octavo. This translation and the notes certainly reveal a sufficient knowledge of languages; but, what is to be regretted, too little hermetic philosophy and hermetic physics. # Entrance.

We wish, therefore, to examine and carefully consider the actions of these brothers; and to sharply and precisely observe their nature and customs; and after we have diligently and carefully weighed everything, let us then judge according to right and fairness: whether these brothers are from God, or from the Devil. We know that the **Holy Spirit** is recognized by His gifts. Let us therefore conduct an investigation to see whether these gifts are to be found within the Brotherhood or not. We read in *1 Corinthians 12:7* that the gifts of the Holy Spirit manifest in everyone for the common good. <note>The author cites the Apostle Paul, who argued that spiritual gifts are not for personal status but for the benefit of the entire community.</note> Thus have *Moses*, *Aaron*, and other patriarchs and prophets received the Holy Spirit not for their own sake, but so that they might teach, exhort, remind, punish, comfort, and, for the worship of the Lord, govern the people of God; for this reason, they openly displayed their spiritual and physical gifts. It is therefore also no small sign of the enjoyment <note>German: *Genusses*; here meaning the participation in or possession of the Spirit's influence.</note> of the Holy Spirit among the brothers that they, too, have publicly presented their **prophecies** and **sciences** <term>sciences: in this context, "Wissenschaften" refers to structured bodies of knowledge, likely including alchemy, medicine, and theology</term> for the **common welfare**. <term>common welfare: or "public good" (Wohlfarth); the author argues that the Brotherhood’s willingness to share knowledge proves their divine intent.</term>

Here too, he walks the everywhere-safe middle path, and does not judge—at least not with decisiveness—matters of which he does not have the proper knowledge and insight, or reliable certainty. We know all too little of the spirit realm, and most people, through their excessive inclination and love for the temporal and physical, make themselves entirely incapable of this science and unworthy of communion with spirits. It is all the more unphilosophical when one mocks those individuals who have been deemed worthy of such knowledge and such communion—which, despite all credible testimony, people refuse to grant them without any basis or counter-proof. ### Concerning Secret Designations.

# Introduction.

...[at time]s proclaims a truth, so that he might gain their trust, and subsequently deceive them all the more easily with his foolish and abominable lies and lead them into an inevitable downfall. He very often concerns himself with the sudden extermination of mankind, which he accomplishes partly in his own person—through thunder and lightning, earthquakes, flooding, the collapse of buildings, storms, and the like—and partly carries out through his servants, such as <term>poisoners: In the 17th century, 'Giftmischer' referred to those using chemical or herbal knowledge for malevolent ends, often associated with dark magic</term>, <term>sorcerers: Practitioners of forbidden arts</term>, <term>necromancers: Those who claim to summon the spirits of the dead</term>, and others of their kind. 

When we now weigh all of this carefully and precisely, we will find that all the works of the Devil are evil and harmful, such that through them no true and useful knowledge is established; instead, nothing but harm, terror, danger, and constant unrest and grief are brought into the world. The reason for this is plain to see: for he is an <note>The author views the Devil as a 'Rächer' (avenger), a theological concept where evil forces are permitted to act as a punishment for those who have strayed from God</note> avenger of the living God and an abominable devastator of everything that has separated itself from God.

# The First Part of the Defense.

In Libavius, many kinds of passions are stirred. For sometimes they are suspicious to him on account of heresy; therefore, in his 19th section, he says: "that reeks of <term>Anabaptist: A radical Protestant movement often associated by critics with social disorder and theological error</term> and <term>Donatist: A schismatic Christian sect from late antiquity that Libavius uses here as a label for those he considers extremists</term> filth." And elsewhere: "what if you increase the heresies instead of diminishing them?" At another time, he most shamefully accuses the Brothers of evil sorcery and superstitious <term>Kabbalah: A mystical tradition involving the search for hidden divine meanings in letters and numbers; here used by Libavius as a pejorative for 'dark' magic</term>. For in the 18th section, it says: "I can smell the abominable sorcery from afar, etc.; therefore, your remedy for the fallen arts is suspicious." Due to the practice of these forbidden arts, he also seeks to slander them in the examination of the 11th, 12th, and 13th arguments, which read as follows:

11. Whoever remains in only one place, yet would like to know the designs of the inhabitants of the Ganges and the <note>original: "Peruaner"</note> Peruvians, let him come into our fellowship.

12. Whoever reads one book, and at once can also read, understand, and remember all other remaining books that ever have been, currently are, or will be in the future—

# On Secret Designations.

...might [wish]; let him join our Brotherhood.

13. Whoever desires—through song or the playing of a musical instrument—to attract not those rocks of <note>Amphion was a figure in Greek mythology who was said to have built the walls of Thebes by moving stones with the music of his lyre.</note> Amphion, but pearls and precious stones; not the beasts of <note>A legendary musician in Greek myth whose music was so beautiful it could charm all living things.</note> Orpheus, but spirits; not <note>The ruler of the underworld in Roman mythology.</note> Pluto from hell, but the mighty potentates of the world; let him enter into the Brotherhood.

Such secrets of nature, it is claimed, cannot be performed or carried out without devilish sorcery and fraudulent <term>necromancy: Historically, the practice of communicating with the dead or spirits to uncover secrets or predict the future.</term>. Therefore, he <note>Referring to the critic, Dr. Andreas Libavius.</note> says in Section 64: "If the matter is so constituted, then you are cursed <term>black magicians: From the German 'Schwarzkünstler,' referring to those who practice forbidden or demonic arts.</term>." # The First Part of the Defense.

...be distinguished from the <term>rabble: The common people or uneducated masses, often viewed by scholars of this era as lacking discernment</term>; by which you would certainly create much benefit if you could invent something permanent in this regard. However, I hardly believe that you would be able to convince the <term>barbers: Used here metaphorically to refer to common tradesmen; the author suggests it is impossible to make everyone follow the same "stroke" or way of thinking</term> that they should all shave according to the same stroke. Frequently he seeks to insult the Brothers with the crime of <term>sedition: Incitement of discontent or rebellion against authority</term>, as in the 28th section. And thus, instead of following a true method of inquiry, he is troubled and confused by other such uncertain appearances and volatile emotions.

For this reason, after a carefully conducted and precise examination, I have divided this treatise of mine into three parts, which are directly and obviously aimed against the doubts, objections, and suspicions of Dr. Libavius. I have set myself the task of defending the propositions of the Brothers in each part against the poor opinions of the slanderers and their worthless and shallow arguments. 

However, I shall by no means use this defense of mine to shame anyone through talkativeness, or to curse and revile the slanderers; rather, I shall merely protect the good name and the reputation of the <term>Society of the Rosy Cross: The Rosicrucians, a mysterious brotherhood of sages, physicians, and alchemists who claimed to possess secret wisdom</term>, against which envy has, to some extent, [begun to] spew its injurious venom—

## Chapter Two.

### Concerning Magic, Cabala, and Astrology.

Because <term>Dr. Libavius: Andreas Libavius (c. 1555–1616), a German physician and chemist who was one of the first and most vocal critics of the Rosicrucian movement</term> seems to use the terms Magic, <term>Cabala: A mystical tradition involving the study of divine mysteries through letters and numbers; here referring to "Christian Cabala," which was popular among Renaissance philosophers</term>, and Astrology—or the art of star-divination—in a negative sense across many parts of his investigation, without making any distinction between their various forms; and since he calls Magic and Cabala "abominable arts" in his 18th section, classifies them along with Astrology as "superstitious arts" in the 24th section, portrays the Brothers as "shameful black magicians" in the 64th, and in the 66th section considers Magic in general and without exception to be false, demonic sorcery or <term>necromancy: The practice of communicating with the spirits of the dead, which was a capital crime in the 17th century</term> (as has already been mentioned above); I have therefore found it useful to discuss the true and proper meanings and distinctions of these arts here, to briefly describe their natures and properties, and thereby to demonstrate both the true intention of the Brotherhood and their innocence against the insults of slanderers and ig- <note>The text ends mid-word with "Un-", which the catchword "wissenden" on the following page completes as "Unwissenden," meaning "ignorant ones."</note>

# The First Part of the Defense.

—norant ones. It must therefore be known that as soon as we banish a certain kind of magic from our schools, we discard the most excellent part of all <term>natural philosophy: In the 17th century, this term (Weltweisheit) referred to the study of nature and the physical universe, the precursor to modern science</term>. It is thus the duty of the wise man to examine a matter precisely before rejecting it without reason. The common opinion in this matter is that the word <note>original: "magia"</note> **magic** stems from the Persian language and, in that tongue, signifies as much as the word <note>original: "sapientia"</note> **wisdom** does among the Latins.

We know from the testimony of ancient and modern philosophers that there are several and very different kinds of magic: one is called natural, another mathematical, yet another deals with the mixing of poisons and the conjuring of the dead, and the last kind is occupied with <term>trickery: The original "Gaukeleyen" refers to sleight-of-hand or deceptive stage magic</term> and sorcerous arts. By **natural magic**, we understand that most hidden and secret part of natural science in which the mystical and secret properties of all natural things are investigated. This is why the royal sages, who (guided by the new star in the East) sought the newborn Christ, were called <note>original: "Magi"</note> **Magi**; because they possessed the highest knowledge of nature—

<meta>catchword: —ledge (completing "Naturkenntniß" or "knowledge of nature")</meta>

# The First Part of the Defense Pamphlet.

In **magic**, **Boethius** <note>Anicius Manlius Severinus Boethius (c. 477–524 AD) was a Roman statesman and philosopher. *) This passage, which our **Fludd** <note>Robert Fludd (1574–1637) was a prominent English physician and mystical philosopher whose works were central to the Rosicrucian debates of the 17th century.</note> likely quoted from memory and according to his own understanding: <note>original Latin: "tuae artis ingenio metalla mugiunt, Diomedes in aere grauius buccinat, aeneus anguis insibilat, aues simulatae cantilenam emittunt," meaning "through the ingenuity of your art metals bellow, Diomedes sounds the trumpet more heavily in bronze, the bronze snake hisses, simulated birds emit a song."</note> actually reads as follows in Cassiodorus: "Metals bellow: the cranes of Diomedes trumpet in bronze: the bronze serpent hisses: simulated birds twitter, (or: chirp) and those things which are not known to have their own voice are proven to emit the sweetness of a song."

the lady climbs into the coach, which the servant closes and then climbs back onto the rear; whereupon the coachman drives on again until reaching the place where the artist had positioned the coach. Such merely mechanical and mathematical feats of art, which strictly speaking do not belong here, serve at least as proof of the various degrees and perfections within them, and the possibility of higher and so-called magical feats of art, or those which at the same time work through the forces of nature. However, certain chemical feats of art and inventions, of which people are now once again boasting, seem to belong here with greater right. 

Such are: Greek fire <note>A legendary incendiary weapon used by the Byzantine Empire, famous for its ability to burn on water.</note>, which was invented by Kallinikus during the time of Emperor Constantine in the year 608; it could be hurled by machines with steel springs and blown through special channels. Instead of extinguishing it, water rather increased its strength and heat; only with wine vinegar could it be extinguished. Its basic mixture was naphtha <note>A flammable liquid hydrocarbon.</note> and sulfur. See *New Course of Chemistry, following the principles of Newton and Stahl. Paris 1723. 8. p. 1.* <note>original: *Nouveau cours de Chymie, suivant les principes de Newton et de Stahl.*</note> In France, *Dupres* <note>Referring to the 18th-century chemist Dupré, who claimed to have rediscovered the secret of Greek fire.</note> had rediscovered it. "A relation, or ratio, is a comparison of two magnitudes of the same kind, in order to form a clear concept of them. The ratio <note>original: "ratio"</note> is either equal <note>original: "aequalitatis"</note>, such as 1 to 1, 2 to 2; or unequal <note>original: "inaequalitatis"</note>, such as 4 to 5, 2 to 3. In an unequal ratio, one considers either the larger number or magnitude against the smaller, or the smaller against the larger. In the first case, it is then called the ratio of greater inequality <note>original: "ratio maioris inaequalitatis"</note>; in the other, the ratio of lesser inequality <note>original: "ratio minoris inaequalitatis"</note>. If only one remains, it is called a **superparticular ratio** <note>original: "proportionem superparticularem"; a ratio where the larger number contains the smaller number once, plus one part of the smaller number (e.g., 3:2, 4:3).</note>; but if more remain, it is called a **superpartient ratio** <note>original: "proportio superpartiens"; a ratio where the larger number contains the smaller number once, plus multiple parts (e.g., 5:3).</note>. In a **superparticular ratio**, the numerator is always called **sesqui-** <note>A Latin prefix meaning "one and a half" or "plus one part."</note>, while the denominator is named according to its number; for example, the ratio of 6 to 5 is called a **one-and-a-fifth ratio** <note>original: "proportio sesqui quinta"</note>, because 5 is contained in 6 once,

## The Second Part of the Defense.

**Square of squares**, <note>In early algebra, a "square of squares" (or biquadrate) refers to a number raised to the fourth power.</note> and other such terms, were composed from the differences of the "more" and the "less." Yet, through these inventions, they have never been able to reach the wisdom and erudition of the ancients contained within the true **art of calculation** <term>Arithmetic</term>, which is hidden, as it were, behind the **proportional clouds**. This is because the secrets of the ancients in the art of calculation seem to have been worn away and destroyed by the **tooth of forgetfulness**, or rather by **envy**, or at the very least have been discovered and revealed only by a few. 

The cause of this may be the imposed **Pythagorean silence**, <note>A reference to the followers of Pythagoras, who were famously sworn to secrecy, keeping their mathematical and philosophical discoveries within their own inner circle.</note> or the "robbery" committed by the ancient **philosophers** <note>original: "Weltweisen." Literally "worldly-wise ones."</note> in this matter, in that they kept the **marrow** of this science for themselves and hid its deep secrets from the eyes of the common and great multitude. Some of the first basic principles of ratios and proportions in the art of calculation have indeed become known to men (as we have already reminded); but where are their true fruits and effects to be found, in whose center the deepest and most divine secrets of the entire art of calculation lie hidden? We also make use of them somewhat, and only superficially, in geometric and musical proportions...

In this way, that mystical Pythagorean science of numbers is being completely eradicated and destroyed. It would therefore be the greatest benefit for the world and for the arts if the nearly eradicated and lost secrets of this most magnificent science were sought out again, and if the declining art were renewed and restored.

The **art of music** <term>Tonkunst: the study of the theory and practice of music</term> follows the **art of calculation** <term>Rechnungskunst: arithmetic</term> in the same way that **medical science** follows **natural philosophy** <term>Weltweisheit: literally "world-wisdom," the standard 18th-century German term for philosophy</term>. For instrumental or vocal music is used by men merely because a shadow of sensual pleasure tickles their ears. The Music of the World and of Man <note>original: "Welt- und Menschenmusik." These terms refer to the classical and esoteric concepts of *musica mundana* (the harmony of the universe) and *musica humana* (the internal harmony of the human body and soul), which were believed to mirror the divine order.</note>, on the other hand, through which the human soul can be raised to the throne of its Creator, is entirely neglected and unknown.

If we properly examine the art of surveying <term>surveying</term> <note>original: "Feldmeßsungskunst." While today this refers to land measurement, in this historical context it refers to the practical application of geometry.</note> commonly used among us, we find that the writings of *Euclid* are filled with many principles, opinions, problems, and theorems. In this art, then, as it is commonly practiced, we are instructed in how to describe regular figures—such as three types of triangles, three types of quadrilaterals, and circles—as well as various types of irregular figures, such as: trapezoids (or scalene quadrilaterals), egg-shaped figures, and others of the same kind, and how to draft them according to their surfaces as well as their threefold physical measurement <note>That is, calculating length, width, and depth/volume.</note>.

...should. Furthermore, we consider various theorems and problems, which concern partly architecture, partly mechanics and the movement of machines, and others which concern the measurement of heights and areas, and the like. However, how far all this is removed from true perfection and the knowledge of the admirable secrets of the art of surveying <term>surveying</term> <note>original: "Feldmeßkunst." In this context, it refers to the broader science of geometry and mechanical engineering as understood by the ancients, rather than just modern land-mapping.</note>, I leave to be judged by all those experienced in this science who are familiar with the machines of Archimedes and their astonishing effects. For through their power and effect, he was able to lift enormous enemy ships with unbelievable force into the air <note>This refers to the "Claw of Archimedes," a defensive weapon described by ancient historians as being used to lift Roman ships out of the water during the Siege of Syracuse (c. 213–212 BCE).</note>, and accomplish many other wonderful things unknown to us through this art. Kindus <note>original: "Kindus." # The Second Part of the Protective Writing.

...[recently] returned from **Italy**, consider it something unseemly and unsocial if they do not frequent prostitutes <note>original: "huren"; in this period, it referred broadly to debauchery or visiting sex workers.</note> and gamble away their money and property with cards and dice. **Hospitality** (this preeminent hallmark of a heavenly love, which otherwise, as far as I can recall, was considered the noblest ornament of **England**) is now almost entirely cast aside, without one being able to provide any valid reason, other than that this kind of "extravagance" (as they call it) is practiced neither by the **French**, the **Spanish**, nor the **Italians**, whose apes we are; and so it is withheld from the poor, and the blessing of God is misused.

His reign is traditionally associated with justice, religion, and prosperity.</note>. Through this, we are tangibly convinced that through the thorough instruction and warning of a wise man, **ethics**, **jurisprudence**, **statecraft**, and **economics** <note>original: <term>Sittenlehre, Rechtsgelahrtheit, Staats- und Haushaltungskunst</term>.</note> can be improved and completely recast. For indeed (as the wise men assert), various arts have greatly increased during these refined and **enlightened times**, and have been enriched and beautified through means that harmonize and cooperate with the nature of the ruling planet.

### The Second Part of the Defense

<page-num>230</page-num>

For the customs of men change along with the times, and the **lower bodies** <note>original: <term>untern Körper</term>. In the pre-modern worldview, this refers to the physical elements and living things on Earth, which were believed to be shaped by the "upper" celestial bodies.</note> receive their qualities through the influences of the upper ones. The **soul** <note>original: <term>Gemüthe</term>. Referring here to the rational mind or the inner spiritual essence of a human being.</note>, however, remains free and is bound to no influence of the stars—provided that it does not, through its union with the body and out of an excessive affection for it, pollute its desires and strivings and direct them falsely. 

Or rather, according to the opinion of the **Platonists** and of **Trismegistus** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, a legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the author of the *Hermetica*, a body of work central to Western esotericism and alchemy.</note>, the soul may completely abandon the spirit that clings to the body and serve only the physical form. In doing so, it prevents the spirit (which is of a heavenly and **fiery-airy nature** <note>In ancient physics, fire and air were considered "active" and "light" elements that rose toward the heavens, whereas earth and water were "passive" and "heavy."</note>) from being subject to the effects of the light ruling in those fiery-airy regions. 

It is also not believable that the **angels**, who are the **movers of the spheres** <note>original: <term>Beweger der Kreise</term>. In medieval cosmology, it was believed that divine intelligences or angels were responsible for maintaining the motion of the planetary orbits.</note>, should be unable to destroy or rearrange anything that nature has ordered. But here too, one must again observe that the effects of a presiding planet will be more powerful in that region of the world which, according to the course of nature, is subject to that ruling planet. 

Therefore, without a proper consideration of these things, it must be absolutely impossible for these **secrets of nature** <note>original: <term>Naturgeheimnisse</term>. This refers to the hidden laws of the universe, such as alchemy, astrology, and natural magic.</note> to be understood by common scholars in a common way...

...to be <note>The text continues on the next page with the word "understood" (original: <term>werden</term>).</note>

In the center of this, therefore, hangs that **sphere of equilibrium** <term>Gleichheitssphäre|A zone of perfect balance in the cosmic hierarchy where opposing forces—like light and matter—reach a state of parity</term>, in which the sun has its movement. From this it is evident that the sun is composed of equal parts water and light, yet in such a way that toward the top, the proportion of light becomes increasingly greater than that of matter; whereas toward the bottom, the proportion of matter likewise gradually exceeds the ratio of light.

From this it is again revealed that, just as with God Himself the pure and most perfect independent **Idea** <note>The divine blueprint or original spiritual archetype</note> and imprint remains free from all creation and matter; on the earth, however, which is opposite and more distant from His throne, there is nothing but formed being and **corporeality**, which is deprived of all light, except for the tiny amount that happens to reach it by chance. Consequently, that circle of equilibrium in which the sun moves occupies the middle position between those two extreme regions; it receives from the **Godhead** a spiritual accord and harmony, and, according to divine pleasure, allows that same harmony to flow down to the earth.

Finally, the **elemental spirit** is a coarser part of the water and consists of three parts matter and only one single part **formal essence** <term>Gestaltwesen|The "form" or spiritual principle that gives a physical body its specific shape and characteristics</term>; therefore, those things contained in this region and area...

# The Third Part of the Concluding Treatise
### Page 252

...ned <note>Completing the word "contained" or "consisting" from the previous page's unfinished sentence regarding elemental things.</note> and consisting of their four degrees <note>In alchemy and early physics, qualities like heat and moisture were measured in four degrees of intensity.</note> are subject to change and are destructible, because their striving, due to an abundance of matter and a lack of the **forming essence** <term>bildenden Wesen|The spiritual or "formal" principle that gives shape, life, and purpose to raw matter</term>, can never be satisfied. 

Therefore, we can make the observation that, just as heat is an essential property that arises and is stirred by the lively effect and movement of light itself; so too does its opposite, **cold**, encompass obscurity, darkness, and material essence. Thus, it is a settled matter that, just as the former property [heat] is recognized as an active effect of light, so too the latter [cold] was drawn out from the bowels and the innermost depths of matter by the excited heat upon the appearance of light. 

From the interaction of these two, **moisture** is produced, the absence of which is nothing other than **dryness**. And so, I have briefly explained the primary causes of both light and the waters and spirits to be found here in this world.

# The Second Chapter.

## On the Essence of Light, its Effects and Hidden Properties in these Inferior Things.

That light is the cause of all effects, just as the moist spirit is the **passive subject** <term>leidende Subject|In traditional philosophy, the 'subject' is the underlying matter that receives an action or form from an active agent—in this case, light acting upon moisture</term>, has been sufficiently proven in the preceding sections. However, nothing has yet been determined regarding the actual nature of this created light. 

In the opinion of Augustine <note>Saint Augustine of Hippo (354–430 AD), whose theological works often addressed the nature of light as both a divine and physical manifestation</note> and others, light is a pure, incombustible fire that grows and multiplies within itself, spreading infinitely everywhere. It encompasses all things, yet is not encompassed; it shines within itself at all times, but is invisible and unknown to other things in and of itself, because it never illuminates in a visible way unless a body, whose components are capable of being illuminated, intervenes. 

From this it follows that the matter of the **middle heaven** <note>In early modern cosmology, the universe was often divided into three realms: the lowest (the elemental earth), the middle (the ethereal or planetary spheres), and the highest (the empyrean or dwelling of the divine)</note>, although it is in itself very luminous and, because of its quantity of light, the highest and...

<note>original: "Licht, Augustinus, Naturgeheimnisse, Materie, Feuer, Himmel, Wirkungen"</note>

<summary>The author begins a new chapter exploring the nature of light, citing St. Augustine to describe it as an infinite, invisible, and incombustible fire that only becomes visible when it strikes a physical body.</summary>
<keywords>essence of light, Augustine, passive subject, middle heaven, incombustible fire, cosmology, optics</keywords>
