### Part Three of the Concluding Treatise.

...takes the first place among composite bodies, yet does not illuminate at night. This fire <note>referring to the "pure, incombustible fire" or celestial light mentioned on the previous page</note> also consists of a simple effect and activity; for besides it, there is nothing more movable, nor anything faster or more powerful in its motion; nothing is finer, nor more penetrating by virtue of its fineness; nothing is more useful or potent, nothing more beautiful, or more uniform in its essence. Finally, there is nothing besides it that could more or less perfect and exalt other things, but also nothing that could more quickly dissolve them and free them from their bonds in a shorter time. <note>In alchemical philosophy, this refers to the process of *solutio* or dissolution, where a substance is broken down into its primary components to be "exalted" or purified.</note>

Upon the greater or lesser present degree of this created being <note>the light/fire</note> also depends the difference between the highest and lowest, and the lowest and middle heaven; and every element of every one of these celestial regions is worsened or improved by its presence or absence. For the further the matter to be formed is removed from the noble forming essence <term>bildenden Weſen|In the Aristotelian tradition, the 'form' or 'forming essence' gives identity and quality to 'matter,' which is otherwise passive and chaotic</term>, the coarser, more impure, darker, and more worthless it is. 

From this arises every distinction among creatures and essences: their perfection and imperfection, rawness and maturity, coarseness and fineness, volatility <note>original: "Flüchtigkeit"</note>

# Of Nature's Secrets.

...and stability, darkness and brilliance, heaviness and lightness; in short, the proportions of all natural things derive solely and exclusively from this source. For this reason, *Hermes Trismegistus* <note>original: *Merkurius Trismegist*; the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the author of the Hermetic Corpus and a foundational figure in alchemy</note> says: All things are measured and weighed by the fiery spirit that animates them. This very perfection constitutes the essence of every creature, both visible and invisible. For the angels (as I have shown above) are endowed with a certain light-filled faculty of cognition <term>Erkenntnißvermögen|The spiritual or intellectual capacity to perceive and understand divine truths</term>, which—though according to different degrees and according to the nature and ability of each—flows through all the boundaries of reason. In the celestial bodies, this spirit has produced an animating power; thus, the lower things <note>referring to the terrestrial realm in the Great Chain of Being</note> receive their life and their potent, light-filled, animating power of reproduction from the stars. In humans, a light-filled radiation of reason prevails. In the other animals, there is a hidden fire, which clearly governs the actions of life and their sensations. In plants, there lies hidden around their centers a certain light-filled essence, which is the cause of their growth and infinite multiplication. Even in minerals and subterranean growths <note>early modern science often viewed minerals as "growing" in the earth like plants</note>, there lies a light-filled, shining spark, which [leads] them toward their...

# The Third Chapter.
## Concerning the Universal Spirit.

In the preceding section, it has been proven that two causes contributed to the existence of the <term>first matter|In alchemy, the "Materia Prima" is the formless, original substance from which all things are created.</term> of all things. Namely: something existing in <term>potentiality|The state of being possible but not yet actualized; the "capacity" to become something.</term>, which was the unformed <term>subject or object|The author uses the German "Subject oder Gegenwurf." In early modern philosophy, "Gegenwurf" was a literal translation of the Latin "objectum," meaning something "thrown against" the mind.</term> of this same unknown matter, and was as it were its dark veil which, as long as it surrounds the matter, allows no impression of any form or shape into it. And this [force], which prevents form and formation...

Mennens’s *Golden Fleece*, book 1, chapter 6, in the *Chemical Theater* <note>original: "Theatrum Chemicum," a famous multi-volume collection of alchemical writings.</note> Volume V, page 288 and following. *The Hermetic A.B.C.*, Part 4, pages 146–157, from *The Mystery of Decomposition and Combustion*. And in the same place, page 167 and following, from the *Microcosmic Preludes*. *Freemasonic Assembly Speeches of the Gold and Rosicrucians*, Amsterdam, 1779, octavo, page 259 and following. *The Light Breaking Forth from Darkness by Itself*, Langensalza, 1772, octavo, page 51 and following.
